```yaml
english_gloss: according to the same argument
observation: At 157a, the argument explicitly applies the same reasoning to the one/many and like/unlike transitions after the motion/rest case.
textual_basis: After the between-state claim for motion and rest, the passage signals parallel application with 'according to the same argument' and extends the pattern to going from one to many and many to one, then from like to unlike and unlike to like.
limits: This observation records the structural pattern of parallel application within 157a. It does not repeat the already recorded motion/rest between-state claim or include the further large/small/equal sequence that follows in the next marker.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: ""
observation: 'Parmenides proposes a thought-experiment in 158c: if one mentally strips away as much as possible from a plurality that does not contain the One, what is stripped away, not partaking of the One, must itself be a plurality and not one; by always examining the "other nature of the form" in this way, whatever one sees of it will always be unlimited in multitude.'
textual_basis: The conditional in 158c explicitly links non-participation in the One with being a multitude, then extends the procedure iteratively to the nature of the form (eidos) considered by itself, yielding an unlimited result.
limits: This observation records the one-over-many structure and the introduction of form-language in a deductive context; it does not evaluate whether the argument succeeds or what metaphysics it implies.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

textual_basis: Three forms of metecho appear in close succession within 159d, each governed by a negation (oudamê). The passage uses participation language to deny rather than affirm a relation between the others and the One.
limits: This observation records the textual fact of negated participation vocabulary at 159d. It does not interpret the metaphysical significance of denied participation, nor does it claim that participation is uniformly negated throughout the Parmenides.
```

```yaml
english_gloss: altered from itself, the discourse, about the one, about something else
observation: At 162d, the argument denies that the one can be altered from itself, on the grounds that if it were altered from itself the discourse would no longer be about the one but about something else. This constraint is explicitly said to apply to both the one-that-is and the one-that-is-not.
textual_basis: The text at 162d states that neither the one-that-is nor the one-that-is-not is altered from itself, because if it were altered from itself the discourse would no longer be about the one but about something else. Parmenides' interlocutor assents with 'correctly' (orthos).
limits: This records the explicit textual argument. It does not assess whether the premise holds for other deduction hypotheses in the dialogue or whether the constraint is valid outside the dialectical exercise.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - στρέφοιτο
  - ἀλλοιοῦται
english_gloss: locomotion, rotation, alteration, moved
observation: "At 162d, the argument eliminates three kinds of motion for the one-that-is-not in sequence: locomotion, rotation in place, and alteration. After denying each, the text summarizes that if the one neither is altered nor rotates in the same place nor moves by locomotion, it cannot be moved at all."
textual_basis: The text at 162d denies locomotion with a brief negative answer, denies rotation by arguing that the same is a being and what is not cannot be in any being, and denies alteration through the discourse-subject argument. The concluding conditional at the end of 162d groups all three denials before inferring immobility.
limits: This notes the tripartite structure of the elimination. It does not claim that these are the only motion types recognized in the dialogue or evaluate whether each denial is sound.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

textual_basis: The passage links alteration with coming-to-be and perishing, infers that absence of alteration removes motion, and then denies standing on the ground that what stands must be in the same place. The conclusion is that what is not neither stands nor moves.
limits: This observation records the local denial of both standing and motion. It does not harmonize this result with other deduction steps.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: Each part of the One contains both one and being, so division recurs indefinitely.

textual_basis: At 142d the One is said to have parts (one and being); at 142e each part is said to contain both "one" and "being," so that the smallest part is made of two parts, and the same reasoning applies always, creating an unending division.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_parmenides_0248
source_work: Parmenides

greek_terms:
  - μετέχειν
  - οὐσίας
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The passage uses participation language to establish that what is not cannot partake of being in any way. At 163c the speaker defines "not being" as complete absence of being, and at 163c-163d concludes that what is not cannot participate in being in any respect. This foundational claim then drives the subsequent deductions about becoming, perishing, alteration, motion, and rest.
textual_basis: |
  At 163c the respondent agrees that "not being" signifies in the plainest sense that the thing in no way and nowhere partakes of being. At 163d the speaker draws the inference that what is not cannot take on or lose being, since it has no share of it at all. The participation vocabulary structures the entire argument.
limits: |
  The observation records the textual use of participation language for non-being; it does not claim a metaphysical doctrine about forms or make any cross-hypothesis synthesis.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_parmenides_0249
source_work: Parmenides
stephanus_span: 163c-163e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/parmenides.txt
  stephanus_span: 163c-163e
  start_marker: 163c
  end_marker: 163e
  start_char: 79211
  end_char: 80446
  text_sha256: 419b9adad4a335f0a5e05608fb112382fd5ef653481dc4858b9235dd0522e1a9
greek_terms:
  - γίγνεσθαι
  - ἀπόλλυσθαι
  - ἀλλοιοῦται
  - κινεῖσθαι
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  A chain of deductions secured by respondent assent moves step by step from non-being's lack of participation in being to the impossibility of becoming and perishing (163d), then to the impossibility of alteration (163d-163e), then to the impossibility of motion (163e), and finally to the impossibility of rest (163e). Each inference follows from the previous one by a shared premise confirmed by the interlocutor.
textual_basis: |
  At 163d the speaker defines becoming and perishing as taking on or losing being, and the respondent agrees. Since what is not shares in no being, it neither becomes nor perishes. At 163d-163e alteration is excluded because alteration would imply becoming or perishing. At 163e motion is excluded because alteration is excluded, and rest is excluded because what is nowhere cannot stand in the same place. Each step receives a confirming response.
limits: |
  The observation records the sequential deduction structure within this span; it does not assess the logical validity of the inferences or connect them to other hypotheses in the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: "The cited passage explicitly states: The one becomes and is older and younger than itself, and also neither older nor younger than itself, since it proceeds through time (becoming older than a younger self), rests at the now (being older and younger simultaneously), and has the same age by being and becoming for an equal time."
textual_basis: The exact cited Greek interval explicitly supplies this attributed proposition.
limits: This observation records only the exact source attribution; it does not infer endorsement, truth, absence, counterevidence, or a general doctrine.
supports_claim_ids:
  - claim_parmenides_0052

```
